\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}=\{w\in\mathcal{W}:\forall c\in SafeActive(\Sigma),\ w\models c\}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-800a-a975-f277ff585211" class="">By construction:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-805b-82ef-d23145da86e0" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
SafeActive(\Sigma)\subseteq Active(\Sigma)
\Rightarrow
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{cons}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806e-be06-e48970f7fc17" class="">This is the basic “envelope is a superset” relationship.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d0-8301-dbaac7ea68a8"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f2-93dd-f8fc41966bfb" class="">2) Rewrite-by-rewrite semantic monotonicity (exact inclusion directions)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806e-beea-f447159d0105" class="">Let . For each rewrite, we prove an inclusion relationship between safe semantics sets (envelopes), and separately between conservative sets.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c6-ad06-d9771d78abce" class="">The end-to-end bound theorem will only require monotonicity of the <strong>safe</strong> semantics (envelope).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-805e-ba21-c4456b9f04fe"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c1-94f4-df87aa597255" class="">2.1 R001 contradiction repair (ctx added)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8044-8f89-c3e16143d407" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Conservative: adds a ctx literal to an active claim → <strong>strengthens</strong> commitments</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8077-9c8a-f8740951437b" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{cons}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{cons}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8060-80e8-e535f5bb092d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Safe: if <code>SafeActive</code> ignores conflict-separation literals, then safe envelope is unchanged:</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80dd-a7cd-d6ee8f16e6dd" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-805e-9b7d-c85c475814fd" class=""><strong>Required design rule:</strong> mark literals introduced by R001 as <code>SepLit</code> and define safe semantics to ignore <code>SepLit</code> in ctx evaluation.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80af-a9ad-f1dc61199bb6"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-804f-92db-dc6b9c9d079a" class="">2.2 R002 cycle break (remove internal edge + insert Primitive boundary)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80fc-89c1-d6194fd7576d" class="">This rewrite reduces dependency constraints (or replaces them with Primitive). Under safe semantics, primitive boundary claims do not add constraints.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802c-ae72-f8136ab4a81e" class="">So:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80e6-8ce2-f484e2e1347b" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8042-9a2a-d79efbcdd00e" class="">This is safe: it reduces commitments.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8044-9184-e244a5493417"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8076-8430-cc8024b83157" class="">2.3 R005 add to allowance (enables MB claim)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b6-b203-f209ac2c13e5" class="">Safe semantics rule: MB claims constrain the envelope only if both:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8099-937f-ec179586899c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">allowed, and</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-800e-a9cf-ebad09769c21" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">gating passes (evidence/metrics or explicit policy)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8086-8a78-d9a68713b0ba" class="">Under the termination design, R005 alone does not fabricate gating evidence, so the MB claim stays non-constraining in <code>SafeActive</code> unless already gated.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a9-88e1-edbe34936791" class="">Thus:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80fd-b0ed-cfdb4b6f31d7" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d4-ba2c-d6f2796ac2e6" class="">To keep the global theorem simple, adopt the strict rule:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8020-8905-ecce06faf53d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">MB claims are excluded from <code>SafeActive</code> always (safe envelope ignores MB entirely).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80fa-881c-ec5aa1550223" class="">Then:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-801c-9812-d5ac63a6b112" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c9-aec5-eccb8c79e6cc"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b3-a3eb-f4b45af228d6" class="">2.4 R009 ownership fix (Unknown → Restricted)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8022-8023-fd5e04fbda9b" class="">Ownership is meta; it does not affect satisfaction .</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8049-8df9-d19bba7528fe" class="">Therefore:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d9-9916-d21dc41e539b" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8014-bc92-c2ce360cf6e0"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80bb-a990-eaf9476f67a0" class="">2.5 R010 budget violation → tag MB</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b7-b218-da36f4511807" class="">This changes how derived interpretations are classified. Safe semantics ignores MB, so envelope unchanged:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8090-894c-fb28c8d29223" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8001-abbe-de7a5344c614"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-808d-b4f8-fafb607afaab" class="">2.6 R007a metric completion</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8045-afa0-d510407de5e5" class="">Metrics are meta unless used as gating constraints in <code>SafeActive</code>. Under the strict safe rule “no metric gating increases constraints,” envelope unchanged:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8086-81f5-ff3271252422" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8031-a2a7-d940b53ea833"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-800b-b88a-cf1bea11850f" class="">2.7 R007b illegal transform → block claim</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-805f-94fa-c40172de7021" class="">Blocked claims are excluded from <code>SafeActive</code>, so blocking weakens constraints → envelope expands:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-805f-92cd-f757efa32127" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8022-8d54-ffab97ae3532"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a4-b775-ec4e90405a4e" class="">2.8 R008 MECE repair</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8053-9f46-f8ef385cad17" class="">MECE objects are meta; safe semantics ignores them:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-800d-85d0-e24a0a896e64" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-808f-855f-c1efe83e5127"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802b-b956-fbb6711b512b" class="">3) Safe envelope monotonicity theorem (per step)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-800e-ad28-ef024f42fd5d" class="">From the above:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8037-9b2d-f226d5ee21f0" class="">For any rewrite step <code>step</code> (which selects some ):</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b5-8192-cc6980489077" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket step(\Sigma)\rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8075-892f-ca019adc339a" class="">This is the key safety property: the system never tightens the safe envelope due to repairs; it only removes or ignores commitments.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-803d-9dc2-e262a7113e5f"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8008-85d9-dcbde7597426" class="">4) End-to-end semantic bound theorem</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a7-a2b3-c837b9a6cfe5" class="">Let:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b5-a7a4-fe18b748fc1d" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\Sigma_0 \xrightarrow{step^N} \Sigma_N</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-808d-9318-d625ec2d5a58" class="">Theorem (Sealed commitments are bounded by initial safe envelope)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b7-88fe-f15e8b157fde" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{cons} \subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_0 \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8030-a379-d55575442675" class="">Proof</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80e7-898f-f442205eeb4a" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li>For any state , commitments are a subset of its own safe envelope:</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-805b-ac9c-ef9ec230a435" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{cons}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8020-bfa7-e6567b7f2e88" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_0 \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_1 \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802f-8b2a-cc4f9b5f5c21" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{cons}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802a-a617-de9a325bd95e" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_0 \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-808b-a187-d4fa8fe21db4" class="">So we use the stronger monotonicity statement:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8046-a6f2-eb5e4cbb5763" class="">Stronger design rule (required)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8065-88d1-d17ae2dac4d8" class="">Define <code>SafeActive(Σ)</code> <strong>only from original evidence-validated claims in </strong>, carried forward by id (and never expanded by rewrites).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8040-9157-df01ec5f1787" class="">That is:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802c-9d66-fa546bb30681" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
SafeActive(\Sigma_t)=SafeBase(\Sigma_0)\cap Active(\Sigma_t)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b2-a004-fb1b6130b67b" class="">Then the safe envelope is <strong>monotone non-increasing</strong> (blocking removes constraints, never adds):</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8009-8756-e30069c20a72" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_t \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_0 \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80e2-9660-e9c46435709c" class="">Now the bound follows immediately:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a6-9caa-f51df6d195aa" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{cons}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_0 \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b5-b52e-d8ef02bea581" class="">This yields exactly the desired guarantee: the final sealed commitments never exceed what was safe according to the original validated base.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d4-8bfb-c9dc772cb654"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80e3-942a-fd7e1f83e6a1" class="">5) What this implies (precise interpretation)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-803f-a3c8-f2c6f5bace51" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Repairs can <strong>remove</strong> unsafe commitments (blocking, MB tagging, primitive boundaries).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ed-8d7c-f33fb5a64326" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Repairs can <strong>separate</strong> contradictions without changing the safe base (ctx separation literals ignored by safe semantics).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d6-a585-ea19a22ee11f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">The final sealed artifact’s committed meaning is always within the initial safe base envelope.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a1-8156-cff1fdb7cf80" class="">This is the correct semantics-level version of “integrity enforcement does not manufacture trust.”</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80df-8707-d2af628ff275"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a3-9c8b-ed16c72a9f5f" class="">6) Lean-style stubs for the end-to-end theorem</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8067-9e92-c0910925d498" class="code code-wrap"><code style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">def SafeBase (s0 : State) : Finset ClaimId := -- validated, export-eligible claim ids from s0

def SafeActive (s0 s : State) : Finset Claim :=
  {c ∈ Active s | c.id ∈ SafeBase s0}

def SemSafe (s0 s : State) : Set World :=
  {w | ∀ c ∈ SafeActive s0 s, SatClaim w c}

def SemCons (s : State) : Set World :=
  {w | ∀ c ∈ Active s, SatClaim w c}

theorem cons_subset_safe :
  ∀ s0 s, SemCons s ⊆ SemSafe s0 s := by
  -- because SafeActive ⊆ Active
  sorry

theorem safe_monotone_nonincreasing :
  ∀ s0 s, SemSafe s0 (step s) ⊆ SemSafe s0 s := by
  -- SafeBase fixed; step can only block/remove from Active, never add to SafeBase
  sorry

theorem sealed_semantic_bound :
  ∀ s0 N,
    let sN := iterate step N s0
    SemCons sN ⊆ SemSafe s0 s0 := by
  intro s0 N;
  -- chain: SemCons sN ⊆ SemSafe s0 sN ⊆ SemSafe s0 s0
  sorry</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ac-b35c-d4954ba93263"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b1-8801-c3736d8aa5f7" class="">7) Only remaining “more” after this</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8082-abbe-dabc7b88a86c" class="">The only substantive extension left is to define <strong>SafeBase(Σ0)</strong> precisely:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8091-85c7-dbef67d733b7" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">which claims qualify as validated (Emp with metrics + range + transform validity)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-804d-94e8-c6c166006fe9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">which ownership statuses allow export</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-801c-ab33-e930c16940b2" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">how allowance interacts (if at all)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806c-9fa5-faaf8d053689" class="">Once SafeBase is formalized, the end-to-end semantic bound becomes a complete theorem with no remaining design degrees of freedom.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8071-8872-c8e0a92b326a" class="">XLV. More: end-to-end semantic bound theorem in the concrete world model (rewrite-by-rewrite monotonicity + sealed safety envelope)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80af-9845-ef539b8a0f00" class="">This completes the semantics track: from a compiled initial state  through rewrites to a sealed , we prove the final commitments are <strong>bounded</strong> by an initial <strong>safe envelope</strong>, using the concrete finite relational+temporal world model from XLIV.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-807c-b0b5-fc9b70ad93a9"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8088-a218-e2cbf715fe85" class="">1) Two concrete semantics sets (now fully defined)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ff-bc19-d23555bbe652" class="">Let  be the set of all worlds satisfying the world axioms:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80e4-bc28-d52b573f078e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">literal complement axioms</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-808b-a133-d9a79e07c4ce" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">predicate complement axioms for neg-pairs</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-809b-92c9-efeb37ada716" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">mutex axioms for mutex object pairs</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8023-a93d-f4686aa71026" class="">1.1 Conservative semantics (commitments)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d0-bfff-cd0ac4bda47a" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{cons}=\{w\in\mathcal{W}:\forall c\in Active(\Sigma),\ w\models c\}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8077-87e5-f65051058fe9" class="">1.2 Safe semantics (envelope)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8024-af5f-d60cb2ee5237" class="">Define <code>SafeActive(Σ)</code> as the subset of claims whose commitments are allowed to constrain the safe envelope:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80dc-8155-d1d45c8168ef" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">exclude blocked claims</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d3-8337-e52eb48e2188" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">exclude MB claims unless they have explicit allowance <strong>and</strong> empirical gating passes (if used)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d4-92ce-c6ef52f794ca" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">optionally exclude “conflict-separation ctx literals” (the ones introduced only to break contradictions)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d8-b69d-c004e7e64acf" class="">Then:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f3-83c0-e9afd451530d" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}=\{w\in\mathcal{W}:\forall c\in SafeActive(\Sigma),\ w\models c\}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8075-9f69-df79fe75797f" class="">By construction:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-801d-8928-f5b8aff1d41f" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
SafeActive(\Sigma)\subseteq Active(\Sigma)
\Rightarrow
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{cons}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d8-8969-e9082147977a" class="">This is the basic “envelope is a superset” relationship.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d0-8267-c0aff4f7a8c2"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8092-81f8-fcc517ebd9b3" class="">2) Rewrite-by-rewrite semantic monotonicity (exact inclusion directions)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8067-ab6a-ca1440395dda" class="">Let . For each rewrite, we prove an inclusion relationship between safe semantics sets (envelopes), and separately between conservative sets.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8047-9274-ea5ce40ddf81" class="">The end-to-end bound theorem will only require monotonicity of the <strong>safe</strong> semantics (envelope).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8049-9d40-ed42bf0e1474"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8053-a960-e60fbed330ad" class="">2.1 R001 contradiction repair (ctx added)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f5-a8b8-f1e042b91fb7" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Conservative: adds a ctx literal to an active claim → <strong>strengthens</strong> commitments</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-801e-a37d-d4402bfdc88a" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{cons}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{cons}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8087-9249-e3df6e7f894f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Safe: if <code>SafeActive</code> ignores conflict-separation literals, then safe envelope is unchanged:</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80cc-ac55-de2e3c16baf6" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-809a-b6cc-e611665f4aed" class=""><strong>Required design rule:</strong> mark literals introduced by R001 as <code>SepLit</code> and define safe semantics to ignore <code>SepLit</code> in ctx evaluation.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-807d-bf2e-ceb9c71e0bde"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a8-ae82-e0346fcfbe7e" class="">2.2 R002 cycle break (remove internal edge + insert Primitive boundary)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-805c-9ad9-dc6abe34c513" class="">This rewrite reduces dependency constraints (or replaces them with Primitive). Under safe semantics, primitive boundary claims do not add constraints.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f6-9dad-e9da49d078f4" class="">So:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8002-9db3-fd8905d7a276" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b7-a1aa-d8f02dbbdf3e" class="">This is safe: it reduces commitments.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c9-86b8-eb151382fd12"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80cd-982c-feeece30a562" class="">2.3 R005 add to allowance (enables MB claim)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8041-877b-fa9ec653b997" class="">Safe semantics rule: MB claims constrain the envelope only if both:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-803b-871f-cdb5da304849" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">allowed, and</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806b-a0e1-f6786fbad492" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">gating passes (evidence/metrics or explicit policy)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8060-95ae-df9a86f3c0c7" class="">Under the termination design, R005 alone does not fabricate gating evidence, so the MB claim stays non-constraining in <code>SafeActive</code> unless already gated.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ce-a532-e767b19c1a0c" class="">Thus:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8035-a79e-ceebfeb05157" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8026-bcf6-f0049eb9cff3" class="">To keep the global theorem simple, adopt the strict rule:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ab-937c-eb7c144d3cdb" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">MB claims are excluded from <code>SafeActive</code> always (safe envelope ignores MB entirely).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f4-9803-dc5a023d738f" class="">Then:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8039-98db-e4e6d5b8e9df" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8069-a47a-e3ca1907d09c"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ce-9e46-f44ef2c667d5" class="">2.4 R009 ownership fix (Unknown → Restricted)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c4-b330-dea5de256282" class="">Ownership is meta; it does not affect satisfaction .</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8092-938a-f55ce09d5880" class="">Therefore:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ef-b784-cd9361de7440" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8065-b3a5-edcaa0da0db1"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8027-a1d5-cc06085c8d83" class="">2.5 R010 budget violation → tag MB</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8099-91d2-e57cd556fc2f" class="">This changes how derived interpretations are classified. Safe semantics ignores MB, so envelope unchanged:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8062-a9db-f3918e1e7c4e" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8034-b2be-ffb7ca86044b"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806d-9e48-c1589c1f3696" class="">2.6 R007a metric completion</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-803a-a775-f644a90d2b96" class="">Metrics are meta unless used as gating constraints in <code>SafeActive</code>. Under the strict safe rule “no metric gating increases constraints,” envelope unchanged:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8084-8f57-f86d40a5dea0" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-808d-9da2-f5ba76193e2d"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c2-97b5-dee6fe77f199" class="">2.7 R007b illegal transform → block claim</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-808a-8972-f09ee4241c23" class="">Blocked claims are excluded from <code>SafeActive</code>, so blocking weakens constraints → envelope expands:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b6-9216-c686a40828cd" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8025-91df-de82b0331649"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8052-bbf9-ea10a7133f30" class="">2.8 R008 MECE repair</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-808e-976c-e1b6de12487c" class="">MECE objects are meta; safe semantics ignores them:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-804c-8c70-eabc7af10b5d" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma&#x27; \rrbracket_{safe}= \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8059-a198-d2b3040b1100"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a5-bab1-ec8122d326da" class="">3) Safe envelope monotonicity theorem (per step)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8029-86ad-fd9def8d03fb" class="">From the above:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80de-bee3-f607b30dbe75" class="">For any rewrite step <code>step</code> (which selects some ):</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806d-8ae3-d2ce013d2ad2" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket step(\Sigma)\rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f5-9be2-c47f56147fdc" class="">This is the key safety property: the system never tightens the safe envelope due to repairs; it only removes or ignores commitments.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8044-9965-e9adae8bdad0"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8071-a532-f3628c7f28b6" class="">4) End-to-end semantic bound theorem</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802a-9cef-c7cd8fee9be9" class="">Let:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80df-98ea-cba7e3ce99f7" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\Sigma_0 \xrightarrow{step^N} \Sigma_N</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8067-b5f4-fa484335f62b" class="">Theorem (Sealed commitments are bounded by initial safe envelope)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-805a-80d7-e4bd58fe39c3" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{cons} \subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_0 \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-809f-87f5-e85460787169" class="">Proof</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8092-b7ce-cd68cb017288" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li>For any state , commitments are a subset of its own safe envelope:</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8034-921d-ca7c44d2324b" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{cons}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-801c-958b-d2a75ecc54fe" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_0 \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_1 \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80e8-a24c-d9038ee342ca" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{cons}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8023-886a-ebcacd59d05f" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_0 \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8025-ba68-c4797534dea9" class="">So we use the stronger monotonicity statement:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b1-ba6a-cfd5b319f4ac" class="">Stronger design rule (required)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b2-b70c-ffaf92f40c34" class="">Define <code>SafeActive(Σ)</code> <strong>only from original evidence-validated claims in </strong>, carried forward by id (and never expanded by rewrites).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806b-9184-e515292a5ef2" class="">That is:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b7-a896-d28d82c8e606" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
SafeActive(\Sigma_t)=SafeBase(\Sigma_0)\cap Active(\Sigma_t)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8081-93c7-eb89de947f55" class="">Then the safe envelope is <strong>monotone non-increasing</strong> (blocking removes constraints, never adds):</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8004-ba8d-d9f4a520d2d3" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_t \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_0 \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8093-b7a3-f8819219f0ea" class="">Now the bound follows immediately:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a3-a981-d4e0f45bd359" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{cons}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_N \rrbracket_{safe}\subseteq \llbracket \Sigma_0 \rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80fa-a791-e3ffffd1b8f5" class="">This yields exactly the desired guarantee: the final sealed commitments never exceed what was safe according to the original validated base.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-801a-b934-c8fde4d76424"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8094-a2f8-fed3a2d62381" class="">5) What this implies (precise interpretation)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8051-8d4e-ca102fdc5e97" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Repairs can <strong>remove</strong> unsafe commitments (blocking, MB tagging, primitive boundaries).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802a-bb8a-cab0b0e1020b" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Repairs can <strong>separate</strong> contradictions without changing the safe base (ctx separation literals ignored by safe semantics).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ec-8649-e257eceb4393" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">The final sealed artifact’s committed meaning is always within the initial safe base envelope.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b8-a1fa-f7add3c72353" class="">This is the correct semantics-level version of “integrity enforcement does not manufacture trust.”</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a0-8067-d879fb9629fc"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-804d-8524-e4ee93b7c1fc" class="">6) Lean-style stubs for the end-to-end theorem</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ed-9784-e69b1763267b" class="code code-wrap"><code style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">def SafeBase (s0 : State) : Finset ClaimId := -- validated, export-eligible claim ids from s0

def SafeActive (s0 s : State) : Finset Claim :=
  {c ∈ Active s | c.id ∈ SafeBase s0}

def SemSafe (s0 s : State) : Set World :=
  {w | ∀ c ∈ SafeActive s0 s, SatClaim w c}

def SemCons (s : State) : Set World :=
  {w | ∀ c ∈ Active s, SatClaim w c}

theorem cons_subset_safe :
  ∀ s0 s, SemCons s ⊆ SemSafe s0 s := by
  -- because SafeActive ⊆ Active
  sorry

theorem safe_monotone_nonincreasing :
  ∀ s0 s, SemSafe s0 (step s) ⊆ SemSafe s0 s := by
  -- SafeBase fixed; step can only block/remove from Active, never add to SafeBase
  sorry

theorem sealed_semantic_bound :
  ∀ s0 N,
    let sN := iterate step N s0
    SemCons sN ⊆ SemSafe s0 s0 := by
  intro s0 N;
  -- chain: SemCons sN ⊆ SemSafe s0 sN ⊆ SemSafe s0 s0
  sorry</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ff-8c96-e77d2d80c5f5"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8061-ba14-dd7cafa37e1a" class="">7) Only remaining “more” after this</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d9-8e18-fae818d1c9a5" class="">The only substantive extension left is to define <strong>SafeBase(Σ0)</strong> precisely:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8080-8ea9-f3eaab527278" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">which claims qualify as validated (Emp with metrics + range + transform validity)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a4-9c2f-f92ba39750b6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">which ownership statuses allow export</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8069-a770-e68f1d6f7a75" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">how allowance interacts (if at all)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8084-bf33-cebc0d4ce440" class="">Once SafeBase is formalized, the end-to-end semantic bound becomes a complete theorem with no remaining design degrees of freedom.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80bc-823d-d005e0b9eb11" class="">XLIV. More: pick a concrete model class (finite relational + temporal traces), define and fully, and prove contradiction-soundness/completeness relative to that model</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8023-90d1-c03662f4cc2f" class="">This instantiates the previously abstract semantics into a fully concrete, decidable model:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-805c-92c3-efb5abf9e41a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">finite subjects</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8068-bfc8-e105debb7df4" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">finite time points</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-807d-8a12-ce8e0c771167" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">predicate interpretations as relations</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8061-997e-e5d3851d482f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">context literals as boolean constraints</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8084-aabd-fcf9dfb2a00d" class="">Then we can state (and in principle prove) strong theorems such as:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8087-9121-d941ebda47a8" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Contradiction soundness:</strong> if UCIA grammar says two claims contradict, no world satisfies both.</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8063-9732-f4696e61a38f" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Contradiction completeness (relative to the fragment):</strong> if no world satisfies both, UCIA grammar detects a contradiction—within the restricted predicate/quantifier fragment.</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-800e-a504-e1c90e37876f"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-803d-b362-c6a22a429018" class="">1) Concrete world model</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a4-924b-d3a6208fea70" class="">Let:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8091-9e58-eee8d7c33e6a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Subjects: finite set</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-803e-886c-c7cd440eeb5c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Time: finite discrete set</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8089-8b68-c44505eaf8b8" class="">A world is:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8064-8ee5-ed63b02cb3f6" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
w = (S,T,\ \mathsf{Allows},\mathsf{Forbids},\mathsf{Increases},\mathsf{Decreases},\ \mathsf{Ctx})</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8004-895f-f6b938dd108d" class="">Where:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d8-8228-d12da7f17b7d" class="">1.1 Predicate relations (per time)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ff-95eb-eb8886c17bff" class="">For each time :</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-801f-abd4-e9f17156db7d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80cb-a2b6-ee0de48816e9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-800c-8f2d-d1081bca6604" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802a-b57c-c3d21d0597d0" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f9-871a-c5371e3f60da" class="">Here  is finite (e.g., Export/NoExport/Promote/NoPromote).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-807c-ab61-f3166fa3f2bf" class="">1.2 Context literal valuation</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8099-b505-e33b009582d3" class="">A valuation function:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8032-83b9-e887f62815e3" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathsf{Ctx} : Lit \to \{0,1\}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8035-b95c-c83bf35098aa" class="">With complement constraint required for well-formed worlds:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80bf-88c8-c3ffa11cf864" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathsf{Ctx}(\neg \ell) = 1 - \mathsf{Ctx}(\ell)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-809b-9fcd-cd0fa84dd281" class="">So worlds are internally consistent w.r.t. literal complements.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806f-b69c-decc57cb4966"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d2-9110-d5ef6af97d30" class="">2) Concrete satisfaction</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8080-ad31-e4492512db3c" class="">Given claim  with NF:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80e6-99e1-e89c5130a99b" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
(subj,\ pred,\ obj,\ quant,\ [t_1,t_2],\ ctx)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-807c-bcc4-eb2536abab60" class="">2.1 Context satisfaction</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c6-ad7a-c0fe1f9e37a0" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
w \models ctx \iff \forall \ell\in ctx,\ \mathsf{Ctx}(\ell)=1</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8042-8811-c3f4197bbb7b" class="">2.2 Time selection</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806c-af23-e06012f9d699" class="">Let interval .</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80aa-9435-e90e011d4cfb" class="">We interpret claims as needing to hold at <strong>some</strong> time in interval (existential over time). This matches common artifact reading and preserves decidability.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-800b-ac29-f5a45ef97fff" class="">Define:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d1-a0f5-cb860c805aa7" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
w \models c \iff (w \models ctx)\ \wedge\ \exists t\in I:\ \mathrm{QuantPred}(w,t,subj,pred,obj,quant)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8066-8c43-cc3633d04db7" class="">2.3 Predicate evaluation</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-800b-bc0b-ce8230034234" class="">Let  be the relation for <code>pred</code> at time .</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b1-8365-f78371fe4eb3" class="">Define:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8084-b7bc-ce8effe4ea52" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ea-aeac-fca291fc7dff" class="">Quantifiers:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80e8-8a14-eae90709c0c6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><code>ALL</code>:</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8066-824d-e299cb81bc34" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathrm{QuantPred} = \forall s\in subj,\ \mathrm{Holds}(w,t,s,obj)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8094-aa22-e31819d80fdc" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathrm{QuantPred} = \exists s\in subj,\ \mathrm{Holds}(w,t,s,obj)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8036-9a06-ff04ce70d749" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathrm{QuantPred} = \neg \exists s\in subj,\ \mathrm{Holds}(w,t,s,obj)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f4-8037-fd64f841f50d" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathrm{QuantPred} = \forall s\in subj,\ \neg \mathrm{Holds}(w,t,s,obj)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8020-a6c7-f32580854a2d" class="">This makes claim satisfaction fully decidable.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-805a-9666-e63d59d23e70"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806d-a6cd-f9a1878d39fe" class="">3) State semantics (conservative)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8058-a717-e6ff43791fc4" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket = \{w : \forall c\in Active(\Sigma),\ w\models c\}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-809d-aff5-effba19be66c" class="">With <code>Active</code> excluding blocked claims.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80e4-bb1e-d53644be9f10"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f5-841f-d3cf30ce6de5" class="">4) Contradiction grammar: soundness theorem (fully concrete)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-807b-8b95-c61951b874e9" class="">Recall UCIA contradiction requires:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8073-82db-fd27f4a4d38f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">subject overlap</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80aa-b8f6-ebc208b9ea13" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">time overlap</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802b-87d1-db646db59774" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">ctx compatibility<br/>and then one of:</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-807f-8909-c68c5573a683" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">predicate negation with same obj</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80fe-847c-d6da702867c1" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">mutex objects under same pred</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8021-ac42-f37730056dfd" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">quantifier opposition (ALL vs EXISTS_NOT) with same pred/obj</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d5-a5c8-d709284a5d74" class="">4.1 Theorem (Soundness)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8035-8bcb-fb9774206935" class="">If , then:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8050-bc21-fa6ae14f8e36" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket c_1 \rrbracket \cap \llbracket c_2 \rrbracket = \varnothing</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8086-bc0a-c1c8634e80f9" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\forall w,\ \neg(w\models c_1 \wedge w\models c_2)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a9-944f-e16c9960443a" class="">Proof sketch (by cases)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802e-87f9-f89115cf0f53" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Neg-pair case:</strong> if and same obj, and both hold at overlapping subject/time, then one requires relation membership while the other requires membership in the neg relation. For soundness you require a world axiom:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8008-8b3a-ea3e47f00046" class=""><strong>Predicate complement axiom</strong> (for Allows/Forbids and Increases/Decreases as complement pairs):</p></div></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b5-ac19-d7070cb1869d" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
  (s,obj)\in \mathsf{Allows}_t \iff (s,obj)\notin \mathsf{Forbids}_t</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8058-a7a1-fffbef831682" class="">Under this axiom, the two claims cannot both be satisfied at the same overlapping witness .</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806d-acab-d52e67407c9d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Mutex case:</strong> if same pred and mutex objects, add a world axiom:</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-807a-9f1c-e5e85d124d3b" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
  \neg\big((s,o_1)\in R_t \wedge (s,o_2)\in R_t\big) \quad \text{for mutex } o_1,o_2</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8038-b8ac-c11dc18844f5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Quant ALL vs EXISTS_NOT:</strong> with same pred/obj and overlapping subject/time, ALL asserts every subject holds; EXISTS_NOT asserts no subject holds; contradiction.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f8-93fa-fe441b46ece2" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Ctx compatibility:</strong> ensures you are not relying on inconsistent literal valuations.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f4-be91-ef84e0cec8ff" class="">Thus grammar contradictions are semantically impossible.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-808c-8675-df6a2a18aad5"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-800b-88d7-c12e146ef5c1" class="">5) Contradiction completeness theorem (relative to the fragment)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8006-aff0-c627931f8db3" class="">Completeness is only true relative to the chosen semantics and fragment restrictions.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8067-a837-d928e3b9ff79" class="">We restrict to claims of the form:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80da-bb76-c05b1d42ce89" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">time interpreted as ∃t in interval</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8003-9a55-d0e70b58aa17" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">ctx as pure conjunction of paired literals</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8094-ba38-fe1de33e8397" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">quantifiers restricted to <code>{ALL, EXISTS_NOT}</code> for completeness proof (or include EXISTS with more work)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8017-bb10-c21307601a59" class="">5.1 Theorem (Completeness, restricted fragment)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8077-ad79-d09fa26a25d2" class="">For any two claims  in the restricted fragment, if:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f1-94d2-f314881b3883" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\forall w,\ \neg(w\models c_1 \wedge w\models c_2)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ab-a40f-ecaa532decfd" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathrm{Con}(c_1,c_2)=1</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d8-967d-c9a0fda0d95d" class="">Proof sketch</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8044-89c9-fb2cd511b0b9" class="">In the restricted fragment, the only sources of unsatisfiability for two single-atom relational constraints with conjunction contexts are exactly:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80fd-a98f-d317428d054b" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">direct predicate complement on same tuple</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-809c-b228-fd40ebc93103" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">mutex tuple constraints</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-807a-84ba-e8a3e7852834" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">ALL vs EXISTS_NOT on same predicate/object tuple</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802e-bf40-d8479c65c92d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">or disjointness (no overlap in subject/time/context), which would allow satisfiability; so unsatisfiable implies overlap + one of the above patterns.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8058-853b-f495fdbce91e" class="">Thus grammar is complete for that fragment.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-804a-84df-e474602db15d"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8067-ab6a-f798e199806b" class="">6) Concrete semantics for literals</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8093-b7f1-f31c5a2710db" class="">We already defined:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d8-afbf-f94355fce9e0" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
L_\ell(w) \equiv \mathsf{Ctx}(\ell)=1</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a7-a03c-d80d0295e9e7" class="">This makes ctx compatibility:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8042-b747-c959d5987a75" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
Compat_\kappa(ctx_1,ctx_2)=1 \iff \neg\exists \ell\in ctx_1:\neg \ell\in ctx_2</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8094-9cb6-d1c2284067b5" class="">So ctx-compatibility is both sound and complete.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c6-b7e7-cb9987e9e93c"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ff-a84e-f0bb3d64652a" class="">7) What “more” can still mean after this (final two expansions)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8008-b1c4-dd6dd43937f5" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Lift completeness beyond the restricted fragment</strong>:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ae-b02f-e71563382bf8" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">include EXISTS, SOME, MOST</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802f-b6a9-c84486bc9b76" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">include different time semantics (∀t vs ∃t)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8045-9a9f-e2f894e58499" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">add subject/object typing</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8047-b0b1-f7f637f57563" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Prove rewrite semantic sandwich end-to-end in this concrete model</strong>:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8040-90bf-cba73f460820" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">classify each rewrite as strengthening/weakening in set-inclusion terms</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c5-8e6a-f5f38df92cd6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">prove sealed state semantics lies within initial safe envelope</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f9-bf29-f53755acec78" class="">If you want to continue with “more” again, the next mathematically non-redundant step is (2): an end-to-end semantics bound theorem using this concrete world model.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8021-97dc-eeb5dfdfc6a9" class="">XLIII. More: semantics layer + semantics-preservation / semantics-bounding theorems for rewrites</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8059-947d-d48407c38ff2" class="">This adds what is still missing: not just “the artifact becomes structurally valid,” but what can be formally said about <strong>meaning</strong> under rewrites.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80eb-afee-e8e08fe41e33" class="">Because “meaning” can be defined in more than one valid way, the correct move is to define two semantics:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30dc5e6f-95bd-801c-a165-dda179bb4fb7" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Conservative semantics</strong> (truth-set style, if you want preservation)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8045-8c11-c26f5bfe002d" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Bounded semantics</strong> (safe over-approximation style, if you want guaranteed safety)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8056-a2e5-e321e597d474" class="">Then we state theorems per rewrite.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80e8-9470-e12d57751dff"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d2-ac1e-e606cf649d8d" class="">1) Semantic domains</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b3-b4b9-ee1476023143" class="">Let the world of interpretations be a set  of “worlds” (models). We keep it abstract.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8099-a996-c4c368f875c2" class="">Each claim  in NF form denotes a constraint on worlds.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-809a-b5f4-cddeb4ff7049" class="">Define satisfaction:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-805c-85f0-e0d344ca8d0a" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
w \models c</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8025-9029-d6939a0a3c4f" class="">Then a state  denotes a set of worlds:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b3-a0b9-e809bd462c24" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket = \{w\in \mathcal{W} : \forall c\in \mathrm{Active}(\Sigma),\ w \models c\}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a6-a911-f9139bd51aaa" class="">Where <code>Active(Σ)</code> are claims not blocked and not explicitly disabled.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-807d-b830-d9b95ec93260"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8034-90ec-d8592276c4e2" class="">2) Claim semantics (NF evaluation)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-807d-a1e1-ff3d5d11294c" class="">Each NF component is interpreted as a predicate over worlds.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802d-8725-fee0b1883af4" class="">Let  be the primitive meaning of <code>pred=p</code> with <code>obj=o</code> in world . This is a parameter of the semantics.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802d-b53f-db0e8b926023" class="">Quantifiers:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80cd-80a2-f1ce9a4ccb33" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><code>ALL</code> means universal constraint within the subject domain</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c9-a4c5-edf5acfeea14" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><code>EXISTS</code> means existence</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-801e-a8cd-c10381f53f9f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><code>EXISTS_NOT</code> means non-existence</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8062-ac5b-e06620550374" class="">Time interval <code>t</code> restricts evaluation to a time slice of .</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-804c-96c2-f2e25e2f1fd1" class="">Context <code>ctx</code> is a conjunction of literal constraints; each literal  maps to a boolean predicate .</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80e5-90df-ebde2b7fe45f" class="">Thus:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8079-a5fa-d933c661ffb9" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
w \models c \iff
\big(\mathrm{QuantSem}(q,subj,p,o,w,t)\big)\ \wedge\ \big(\bigwedge_{\ell\in ctx} L_\ell(w)\big)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806a-8dde-d4496ea4bcbd"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f8-ad9d-d31d104021c8" class="">3) Two semantics modes</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c7-97f4-da2c72a1df35" class="">3.1 Conservative semantics (exact, preservation target)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c7-9ed0-f971c8e509c7" class="">Use the definition above directly:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80eb-be1f-f132133fb571" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{cons} = \{w:\forall c\in Active(\Sigma),\ w\models c\}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8059-a5fb-e37850509ca4" class="">3.2 Bounded safety semantics (over-approx, safety target)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8094-8056-f3b56853acdf" class="">When the system inserts primitives, blocks claims, or tags MB, we interpret those as <strong>weakening</strong> constraints rather than strengthening.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ce-aa79-dfbc6985da5d" class="">Define:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-808a-aeae-c0819c1bde75" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">blocked claims contribute nothing to constraints</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-806c-aa5d-febbc8e48ab5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">MB claims contribute nothing unless allowed</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8081-b6b8-eebae620279c" class="">This yields a larger world set (less restrictive):</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f0-b43c-f59f919983c7" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma \rrbracket_{safe} = \{w:\forall c\in SafeActive(\Sigma),\ w\models c\}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a3-b9aa-e0c918a69b62"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8072-9b94-da1d07687140" class="">4) Semantic effect classification of rewrites</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80cd-9771-cad4dadbdf47" class="">Each rewrite  is either:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8038-ad5f-e0420e764d04" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>strengthening</strong> (shrinks world set)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8009-9882-cee8e906c6bf" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>weakening</strong> (expands world set)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8004-9138-fef950a95222" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>refactoring</strong> (world set preserved)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ca-b375-e8f873a81dae" class="">Formally:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-800c-a40a-e25e8ccf506c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">strengthening:</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80bb-a8b5-fb6fe5ba89d9" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \mathcal{R}_j(\Sigma)\rrbracket \subseteq \llbracket \Sigma\rrbracket</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a6-8e66-fa49ae459945" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma\rrbracket \subseteq \llbracket \mathcal{R}_j(\Sigma)\rrbracket</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8098-b6e3-ebb95f86f0f4" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \mathcal{R}_j(\Sigma)\rrbracket = \llbracket \Sigma\rrbracket</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8043-937e-dd589570d133"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c8-8a6c-ee725b80ecc3" class="">5) Theorems per rewrite (what is true, exactly)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8035-8bd6-d6d9e60e420c" class="">5.1 R001 (add ctx literal to break contradiction)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ff-9d56-f4e814b5704d" class="">R001 adds a context constraint to one claim, which strengthens that claim.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d8-bcf1-d6bda64749a1" class="">Thus under conservative semantics:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b4-b8f3-eacb3a928840" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \mathcal{R}_{001}(\Sigma)\rrbracket_{cons} \subseteq \llbracket \Sigma\rrbracket_{cons}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-808d-8ddc-f9817153282b" class="">This is not preservation; it is strengthening.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80ee-a9a3-f961edd46f43" class=""><strong>Interpretation:</strong> contradiction resolution is achieved by narrowing meaning (excluding some worlds).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8024-97e4-f2f6e4034606" class="">Under safe semantics, you can reframe it as bounded:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d9-9527-f871e8799a40" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">If ctx literal is of the form <code>Unmeasured</code> and your safe semantics ignores that literal, then it becomes semantics-preserving in safe mode.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8084-b1b0-c249310268d1" class="">So:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-802d-8129-da0981597313" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \mathcal{R}_{001}(\Sigma)\rrbracket_{safe} = \llbracket \Sigma\rrbracket_{safe}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8071-9f09-dbd66c50e871" class="">That is a design choice.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80fd-854c-e0a3a3f02064"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80c7-8453-e333b9086ac1" class="">5.2 R002 (break cycle by inserting Primitive boundary)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8053-97c1-c4f3a4c54592" class="">Inserting a primitive boundary is weakening (it replaces a dependency constraint with an unconstrained primitive).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8065-9de2-c53b7688645a" class="">Thus:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80d5-9500-cd4434973305" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \Sigma\rrbracket_{cons} \subseteq \llbracket \mathcal{R}_{002}(\Sigma)\rrbracket_{cons}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80a5-bbe7-f4ca652a8c10" class=""><strong>Interpretation:</strong> cycle breaking removes information.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8064-951d-f5a848c60b3c" class="">Safe semantics: also weakening or equal depending on whether dependencies were already ignored.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8094-8c29-e5f184ca030a"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8095-901e-d06d62d92c33" class="">5.3 R005 (add MB claim to allowance set)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80f0-9c0b-d2911d3fbd21" class="">This does not change claim meaning; it changes whether MB claims are permitted.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80fb-9cef-c9f5533af1e5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Conservative semantics: allowance affects which claims are active, so enabling an MB claim is strengthening (adds constraints).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8077-ae42-f4d7dfe43a7b" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\llbracket \mathcal{R}_{005}(\Sigma)\rrbracket_{cons} \subseteq \llbracket \Sigma\rrbracket_{cons}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30dc5e6f-95bd-80b0-bda0-f18a45300c02" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Safe semantics: if MB claims are ignored unless validated, then enabling allowance may still not affect safe semantics:</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30dc5e6f-95bd-8019-8eda-ed05416f7dc0" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
